Let's write robust functions:

`_normalize(p)`: clip finite, max(0), sum, return p/sum or None.

`_score(p)`:
```
p=np.asarray(p)
s=p.sum()
if s<=0: inf
p=p/s
c=_conv_full(p,p)
return 2*len(p)*c.max()
```

`_conv_full(a,b)`: use direct if small else rfft L=next_pow2(len(a)+len(b)-1). Ensure output length real; small imaginary ignore.

`_weighted_A(q,p)`:
- n=len(p); L=next_pow2(2*n-1)
- Q=np.zeros(L); Q[:len(q)]=q
- P=np.zeros(L); P[:n]=p
- A=np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(Q,L)*np.conj(np.fft.rfft(P,L)), L)[:n]
- sym? If q symmetric? Not always; use only first n.
But we need A_i=∑ q_{i+k} p_k. The formula with conj(P) seems correct. Let's verify with n=2, Q=[q0,q1,q2], P=[p0,p1]. rfft length L. IFFT(Xconj(Y))[i]=∑ x[(i+k) mod L] y[k]? Need confirm numpy `irfft(fft(x)*conj(fft(y)))` gives circular cross-correlation `xcorr` such that `xcorr[n] = ∑_k x[k+n] conj(y[k])`? For real, yes. So A OK. Could test small mentally: x=[1,2], y=[3,4], N=2; xcorr[0]=1*3+2*4=11; x*conj(y) DFT corresponds ∑ x[m] y[k] e^{-2πi(k-m)/N}; inverse at n=0 ∑ x[k]y[k]=11; n=1 ∑ x[k] y[k-1]? x[0]y[1]+x[1]y[0]=10. Desired A0=∑q_i p_i? Our q length i+k gives A0=q0p0+q1p1, yes xcorr[0]. Good.
- If q sum=1, A values maybe.
For maybe q not normalized? We can normalize.

- theta = np.asarray(theta, float). Clip to [-80,80] for stability? For gradient, if clipped, gradient at bounds? Use raw after clip but if we clip inside, gradient not consistent. `_freq_flatten(p, alpha, L)`:
- c=conv(p,p); cmax = c.max(); if cmax<=0 return.

Let's solve: On circle length 2, support [0,1]. Want (f*f)(t)=1/2 for all t. Fourier: F(2πk) =? The constant 1/2 has nonzero only k=0: F(k)^2 =? At k=0, F(0)=1. For k≠0, F(k)^2=0 => F(k)=0. So F zero for all nonzero integer frequencies. But f supported [0,1] with all nonzero Fourier coefficients zero would be constant on circle? Impossible (Uniqueness). Thus exact flat impossible. Lower bound >1. The minimum is an approximation: choose f such that its Fourier coefficients nonzero only where allowed (support half) with small magnitude. This is a signal design problem: minimize L∞ time coefficient given F(0)=1 and f nonnegative support half. Complex signed solutions maybe: choose F(k)=1 for some, etc. Nonnegative adds.

This Fourier perspective is powerful. Let M=2n-1 odd (approx 2n). Zero-padded sequence p length n has DFT P_k (k=0..M-1). Its cyclic conv c_k = (1/M)∑ |P_j? Actually (p*p)_k = (1/M)∑ P_l P_{k-l} (not | |) because convolution not correlation. But since same sequence, P_l P_{k-l}. For real p, P_{M-l}=conj(P_l). Nonnegative? The Fourier coefficients of c are products P_l P_{k-l}. We want c_k small. Sum c=1. If we can make P_l such that all low-order trigonometric product coefficients small. This is like designing a nonnegative polynomial P(z) with small coefficients of P(z)^2.

If p are nonnegative, P_l can have complex phases. For p supported in half-cycle and positive, maybe P has constrained phase (positive definite?) Not exactly.

Maybe use **random phases** with prescribed magnitude to make c small (flat). If choose P(z) with random independent phase in each frequency and choose p=?? But p must be real nonnegative. If we choose P such that inverse DFT is nonnegative, that's spectral factor with nonnegative constraint. Could use phase retrieval / projection.

Maybe better: Since p nonnegative support half, its DFT has special property: P(z) is an analytic polynomial. The products P_l P_{k-l} are autocorrelation coefficients. There are **Barker/Bose-Chowla** sequences for polynomial P(z) with coefficients ±1 having flat autocorrelation. Our coefficients nonnegative, but could take P(z)=(1+Q(z))/2 where Q has ±1. For a signed sequence s_i with flat autocorrelation (Barker/m-sequence), the nonnegative p=(1+s)/2 may have good conv. Let's analyze more.

Let s_i ∈ {±1}, sum maybe 0. p_i=(1+s_i)/2 nonnegative 0/1. Then P_p = (M? )? For length n, P_p=(1+P_s)/2. c_p = 1/4(c_1 + 2 c_{1,s} + c_s), where c_1 is conv of all-ones (triangular), c_{1,s} is prefix sum-like, c_s is signed conv. For a signed sequence with low linear autocorrelation, c_s small except near 0? The triangular c_1 peak center. Can c_{1,s} cancel it? For m-sequence, prefix sums? The indicator support of 1s has pair sums. If signed autocorrelation negative can reduce. Maybe binary Barker-like with density 1/2 gives score near? For random 0/1 score 2; with signed structure maybe lower.

What is optimal binary sequence? This is related to **minimum energy sequences** for optical CDMA; nonnegative 0/1 with low cross? Maybe known "perfect sequences". The smallest score for n=2 was 1.777 (sequence [2/3,1/3] not binary). For large n, maybe 1.5.

Could use **simulated annealing on binary sequence** plus weights. However continuous parent likely better.

Let's check possibility of exact construction via **trigonometric polynomial with small coefficients** from **Bose-Chowla**: Let P(z)=∑_{i∈S} z^i where S is a Sidon set in Z_M. Then P(z)P(z^{-1}) has coefficients for differences (not sums) small. For sums P(z)^2. If S is a **basis of order 2 with balanced representations**, P^2 coefficients bounded. There exist **perfect difference sets** for differences; for sums, if we take S symmetric about 0 (i.e., if i in S then -i in S), then sums i+j relate to differences? In cyclic group, P(z)^2 coefficients count sums. If S has cardinal k and is a **relative difference set** maybe sums unbalanced. But if S is a **B_2 sequence** with bounded representation, can choose k ~√M. Score ~c M/k^2 ~ constant if max reps ~1? But k~√M, score ~O(1). For M=2n, target 1.5 need max reps ≤1.5, mean reps=M/k^2? Wait normalized equal weights: c_m = reps_m/k^2 (for cyclic sum over M, total reps k^2). Average=1/M. Score = M*max_reps/k^2. Need max_reps ≤1.5*k^2/M. If k=√M, RHS=1.5, so max reps must be ≤1 (for equal weights) but mean=1, possible only if all reps=1 (perfect multiplicative Sidon covering all M), impossible if k^2=M and all sums distinct? A permutation of group would be all ordered pairs bijective -> k^2=M and S+S bijective. That means S is a **perfect 1-factorization?** If S+S covers each element exactly once, then |S|^2=M. For finite abelian groups, such a set S with |S|^2=|G| and S+S bijective? That would be a **digital line** or **complete sequence**? If |S|=√M, S+S =G with unique representation, S is a **group square root**? For cyclic group odd, can we have a set of size √M such that every element has unique sum of two elements from S? That would be a **Costas array**? Actually if |S|=r, there are r^2 ordered pairs. Unique sums mod M means mapping S×S→G bijective. This is a **finite field square**? Let S be the set of elements of a subfield? If G additive of F_{r^2}, choose S=F_r as additive subgroup. S+S=F_r (not whole G) for r^2? Not. If S is a complete set of residues mod r? Unique representation as sum of two elements of S mod M? This is a **perfect difference set**? For additive group of order q^2, a set of size q with unique sums would be a **Latin square transversal**? It means S is a **quasigroup operation table**. Does such subset exist in cyclic group? It would imply polynomial P(z)^2 = 1+z+...+z^{M-1} = (z^M-1)/(z-1). As before impossible for polynomial factor over complex? But cyclic group polynomial P(z)^2 having all coefficients 1. This requires P a square root of R_2(z). For M odd, R_2(z) not a square in C[z]/(z^M-1)? Its roots all simple except 0, P^2 would have double roots. Since R has no repeated roots, impossible. So unique sums impossible. For non-cyclic maybe? But our group is cyclic due to sequence lag.

If allow weights and k not √M, optimal max reps maybe >1. This is additive combinatorial design. Could use **Welch bound** for Welch-like sequences: For M cyclic, p nonnegative support half. The average of squared Fourier? Parseval: ∑ |P_k|^2 = M ∑ p_i^2 ≥1? Actually p sum? If weights. The c_k are inverse products. Minimax lower bound via Fourier: Since p nonnegative support half, |P_k|^2 has certain energies. Maybe target 1.5 from optimal Fourier concentration.

Maybe fixed n=3072, parent length. Could approximate via **IRLS/active set**.

Let's return to practical: We need produce a program likely to improve. We can combine many methods without knowing exact optimum. More computation from parent may push to target. We need avoid using too much time on unlikely methods. Let's prioritize:
- L-BFGS smooth local from parent (major improvement potential).
- Exact active multiplicative line search (polishing).
- Multi-length and multi-start (escape local).
- Spectral flatten (novel basin).

The previous program did multiplicative only; L-BFGS is key new. Also maybe use **high-precision exact active set and binary peak positioning** to reduce max below.

def _next_pow2(x): ...
def _softplus? no.
def _sanitize(p): ...
def _softmax(theta): maxθ=np.max(theta); u=np.exp(theta-maxθ); s=u.sum(); if s<=0: return uniform; return u/s
def _conv_full(a,b):
    nout=len(a)+len(b)-1
    if nout==0: return zeros
    if len(a)*len(b)<=200000: return np.convolve(a,b)
    L=_next_pow2(nout)
    out=np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(a,L)*np.fft.rfft(b,L),L)
    return out[:nout]
```
For repeated conv on same n, passing L maybe to reduce next_pow2. Add optional L.

def _conv_self(a,L=None): ...

`_score(p)`:
```
p=np.asarray(p,float)
p=np.where(np.isfinite(p),p,0); p=np.maximum(p,0); s=p.sum()
if s<1e-300: return inf
p=p/s
n=len(p)
L=_next_pow2(2*n-1) maybe passed
c=_conv_self(p,L)
return 2.0*n*float(c.max())
```
Need accept full sequence not normalized? The official clamps negative and caps 1000, sum can be any. Score invariant to positive scaling (as long sum>0). Our score normalized. Good.

`_weighted_A(q,p,L=None)`:
```
n=len(p); L=L or _next_pow2(2*n-1)
Q=np.zeros(L); Q[:len(q)]=q
P=np.zeros(L); P[:n]=p
# R = irfft(rfft(Q,L) * conj(rfft(P,L)))
return np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(Q,L)*np.conj(np.fft.rfft(P,L)), L)[:n]
```
Need ensure if q length >L no; q length 2n-1, L>=2n-1.

`_smooth_f_g(theta, beta, reg=0.0):`
```
theta=np.asarray(theta,float)
theta=np.where(np.isfinite(theta),theta,0.0)
maxt=np.max(theta); u=np.exp(theta-maxt); s=u.sum(); if s<=0: p=uniform
 p=u/s
 n=len(p); L=_next_pow2(2*n-1)
 c=_conv_self(p,L)
 d=n*c
 if beta <= 1.0: q=np.ones_like(d)/len(d); lse=np.mean(d) #? If beta small use mean? But gradient? We can handle beta small by q uniform, f=mean d, grad via A = mean? For beta=1 okay. But we call beta>1.
 shift=d.max()
 z=beta*(d-shift)
 z=np.clip(z, -700, 700)
 e=np.exp(z); es=e.sum()
 if es<1e-300: q=zeros; q[argmax]=1; else q=e/es
 lse = shift + math.log(es)/beta
 # if reg: f += 0.5*reg*dot(theta,theta)
 A=_weighted_A(q,p,L)
 meanA = float(np.dot(p,A))
 g = 2.0*beta*p*(A - meanA)
 if reg: g += reg*theta
 # scale? f finite
 return f,g
```
Check meanA = ∑p_i A_i = ∑q_j c_j? c_j =∑p_i p_{j-i}; A_i=∑q_j p_{j-i}; dot =∑q_j∑p_i p_{j-i}=∑q_j c_j. Also Dq/n. Good.
If q has only max, A_i=p_{m*-i}; meanA= c_m? dot pA = c_m? c_m=∑p_i p_{m-i}. Yes.

Wait derivative of lse βd with q: ∂f/∂p_i = 2β A_i. If using p (sum1) extension? For softmax gradient: gθ_i = p_i(2β A_i - 2β∑p A) =2βp_i(A-meanA). Good. f = lse (βF). Consistent.
If beta small, q uniform, f lse ≈ dmax + log(L)/beta -? Not mean. Could just compute. For beta=10 fine.

Regularization: if theta has large values, f includes 0.5 reg sumθ². But gradient uses raw theta; if we use bounds and no clip, consistent. We'll call with raw theta. To avoid overflow, inside softmax we clip `theta_clip = np.clip(theta, -80, 80)`? If we clip, derivative for v beyond clip zero, inconsistent but small. Better use bounds [-60,60]; no need clip except for numerical if line search exceeds? L-BFGS-B respects bounds. In custom active, we can clip.

Maybe `bounds` on theta: [-25,25] to keep p not too extreme. But optimum might need zeros/head high? Parent maybe smooth; 25 diff enough (p ratio 7e10). Use [-35,35]. With n large, p min ~6e-16, okay.

  - n<=8192: 150
  - n<=16384: 80
  - else 40
But multiple runs; we can call multiple betas with 50 each.
Options: `{'maxiter':maxiter, 'ftol':1e-12, 'gtol':1e-8, 'maxls':20}`.
  - res = minimize(...)
  - if res.success? ignore.
  - theta=res.x; p=_softmax(theta); score=_score(p); update.
- Also if no improvement but p changed, continue? Use best p to center? Could restart from best after each beta.

`_active_refine(p, rng, time_budget, verbose=False):`
Let's design robust.
```
def _active_refine(p0, rng, time_budget, quick=False):
    p=_sanitize(p0)
    if p is None: return None, inf
    n=len(p)
    L=_next_pow2(2*n-1)
    theta=np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300)); theta-=theta.mean()
    best=p.copy(); best_score=_score(p,L?)
    cur_theta=theta.copy()
    deadline=time.monotonic()+time_budget
    stall=0
    alphas=[0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4,0.8,1.5,3.0]
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
       p=_softmax(cur_theta); score=_score(p,L?)
       if score<best_score: update best, cur_theta? Actually p from theta; if improved, best=p, best_score=score.
       # compute d and q modes
       c=_conv_self(p,L); d=n*c; peak=d.max()
       if not finite: break
       # weights
       for mode in [0,1,2]:
          if time break
          if mode==0: q=softmax(random beta 100-5000)
          elif mode==1: top K with weights: K=random [1,2,4,8,16,32,64,128]; weights descending exp(-rank/tau)
          else: single peak and maybe active from top index; or use negative? 
          A=_weighted_A(q,p,L)
          base=A - meanA
          # maybe use one-sided high: dir=np.maximum(base,0) - mean? but keep base.
          norm=np.max(np.abs(base)); if norm small continue
          direction=base/norm
          # line search
          improved=False
          for a in alphas:
              th=cur_theta - a*direction
              # maybe if a causes large negative? OK
              pt=_softmax(th)
              sc=_score(pt,L)
              if sc<score-1e-12:
                  cur_theta=th; score=sc; improved=True
                  if sc<best_score: best=pt.copy(); best_score=sc
                  break
          if improved: stall=0
          else: stall +=1
       if stall> some:
          # random smooth perturbation
          if best_score finite and score>best_score*1.02: cur_theta=best_theta
          # Add noise
          th = cur_theta + rng.normal(0, noise_std, n); th-=mean; score_try; if sc < score*1.05 accept
          stall=0
    return best,best_score
```
Need keep best_theta to restore. When best updated, best_theta=cur_theta.copy() (if p from theta). At start best_theta=theta.
But line search sequence alphas: We may try small to large. If a large improves over current, accept and break; maybe a larger later also improves, but next iterations can. Could do best among several without early break to choose best, but more eval. Early break okay.

For exact score, `_score(pt,L)` with L same. Need ensure L matches n.

Weight softmax for q:

def _softmax_lags(d,beta):
 shift=d.max(); z=beta*(d-shift); clip; e; if sum>0 normalize else onehot max
```
`d` symmetric; q sum1.

Top K mode:

K = int(min(2*n-1, rng.integers(1,257)))? maybe [1,2,4,...,256]
idx = np.argpartition(d, -K)[-K:]
# assign weights by loguniform or 1/(rank+1)
vals=sorted(d[idx], reverse)
w=np.arange(K); w=w/(w.sum()) descending. q[idx_positions]=w.

Single peak mode: q zero, q[argmax d]=1. This can reduce main peak but may create next.

Direction normalization: For q top K, A may be smooth; using meanA (dot(p,A)) is not mean over A, but correct gradient mean. If A distribution, some negative direction. Fine.

Could also use `direction = A - np.quantile(A,0.5)` for one-sided. We'll include random choice:
- 50% base (A - dot)
- 20% A - median(A)
- 20% positive part: Apos = np.maximum(A - mean, 0); direction = Apos - Apos.sum()*? Need make sum relative? For θ update, can be any; line search. Use Apos (no mean) only reduces high A, not increase low, mass moves to where? Softmax shift with no sum-zero will scale all p? If θ -= α Apos, total softmax normalized; coordinates not in Apos unchanged relative, effectively high reduced mass redistributed to all low? Good. But it has no zero mean, shifts all. Could be okay. Normalize by max.
- 10% top-pair reduction: For peak m, A_i=p_{m-i}. direction = np.where(A > crit, A-crit, 0) maybe.

But too many modes may complicate. Keep simple.

Exact score evaluations: We can add a fast **subgradient estimate** without full conv for direction candidates? Need exact for acceptance. Use conv.

`_spectral_refine(p, rng, time_budget)`:
- Start p. For iterations:
  - c=conv; cmax; if cmax low.
  - For several alpha_f in [1.2,1.5,2,3,5,10]: C_new = cmax * (c/cmax)**(1/alpha_f)
    To ensure C symmetric? c already symmetric. PSD? Compute factor = sqrt(rfft(C_new)). But rfft(C_new) may have negative real. Use magnitude? We can use `F=np.fft.rfft(C_new,L); factor = np.abs(F)**0.5 * np.exp(0.5j*np.angle(F))` = sqrt complex. If F has negative, angle handles.
    a=np.fft.irfft(factor,L)[:n]; if negative frac >0.4 skip; a=max(a,0); normalize; score; if improve accept; perhaps small perturbation.
- This may be expensive; limited to early global.

But maybe unnecessary.

Potential code for `_topk_weights(d, K, rng)`:
```
def _topk_weights(d, K):
    d=np.asarray(d)
    if K<=1:
       q=np.zeros_like(d); q[np.argmax(d)]=1; return q
    K=min(K,len(d))
    idx=np.argpartition(d, -K)[-K:]
    vals=d[idx]
    order=np.argsort(vals)[::-1]
    idx_sorted=idx[order]
    ranks=np.arange(K, dtype=float)
    # weights exponential
    tau=max(K/3.0,1.0)
    w=np.exp(-ranks/tau)
    w=w/w.sum()
    q=np.zeros_like(d)
    q[idx_sorted]=w
    return q
```
No lambdas.

`_softmax_lags(d,beta)`:
```
def _softmax_lags(d,beta):
    d=np.asarray(d,float)
    shift=d.max()
    z=beta*(d-shift)
    z=np.clip(z,-700,700)
    e=np.exp(z)
    es=e.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(es) or es<=0:
       q=np.zeros_like(d); q[d.argmax()]=1; return q
    return e/es
```

`_random_soft_weights(d,r,beta)`.

`_run_lbfgs` signature:
```
def _run_lbfgs(p0, beta_schedule, time_budget, verbose=False, maxiter_cap=300):
   p=_sanitize(p0)
   n=len(p); L=...
   theta=log...
   best_p=p.copy(); best_score=_score_L(p,L)
   deadline=time.monotonic()+time_budget
   if minimize is None: return best_p,best_score
   # calibrate cost once
   cost=_gradient_cost(theta, 100.0, L)
   for beta in beta_schedule:
      if time.monotonic()>=deadline: break
      rem=deadline-time.monotonic()
      # choose maxiter based on rem and cost, but not exceed maxiter_cap
      maxiter=int(rem / (8.0*cost)); maxiter=max(10, min(maxiter_cap,maxiter))
      bounds=[(-50.0,50.0)]*n
      try:
         res=minimize(_smooth_f_g, theta, method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, args=(beta,1e-10,L), bounds=bounds, options={'maxiter':maxiter,'ftol':1e-12,'gtol':1e-8,'maxls':10,'maxcor':10})
         theta=res.x
         p=_softmax(theta)
         sc=_score_L(p,L)
         if sc<best_score: best_p=p.copy(); best_score=sc
      except Exception as e:
         print('L-BFGS error', e)
   # after run, return best
   return best_p,best_score
```
This may allocate bounds each beta; expensive for large n. Create once outside.
But `minimize` may call fun at theta with bounds; okay. If `res.x` not finite, handle.

def _active_refine(p0, rng, time_budget, verbose=False, small_budget=False):
    p=_sanitize(p0); if None return
    n=len(p); L=...
    theta=np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300)); theta-=mean
    best_p=p.copy(); best_score=_score_L(p,L); best_theta=theta.copy()
    cur_theta=theta.copy(); cur_score=best_score
    alphas_base = [0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,1.0,2.0,5.0]
    sp_base = [0.5,1.0,2.0,5.0]? Since direction normalized by max abs, alpha correspond θ change max. For p ratio change e^alpha; alpha up to 5 huge. Use [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,1.0,2.0,4.0]
    deadline=...
    stall=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       p=_softmax(cur_theta); sc=_score_L(p,L)
       if sc < best_score - 1e-12: best...
       # compute d
       c=_conv_self(p,L); d=n*c; peak=d.max()
       if not finite: break
       # choose 2 directions
       directions=[]
       # softmax weights
       b = float(10.0**rng.uniform(1.0, 4.0)) # 10..10000
       q=_softmax_lags(d,b)
       A=_weighted_A(q,p,L); dir=A - dot(p,A)
       if maxabs>1e-12: directions.append(dir/maxabs)
       # topK
       K = int(rng.choice([1,2,4,8,16,32,64,128,256]))
       q2=_topk_weights(d,K)
       A2=_weighted_A(q2,p,L); dir2=A2-dot(p,A2)
       if maxabs>1e-12: directions.append(...)
       # one-sided peak
       if rng.random()<0.4:
          mstar=int(np.argmax(d))
          q3=np.zeros_like(d); q3[mstar]=1
          A3=_weighted_A(q3,p,L)
          dir3=np.maximum(A3 - np.mean(A3),0)
          # subtract small mean? no
          if maxabs>1e-12: directions.append(dir3/maxabs)
       improved=False
       for dir in directions:
          # try alphas: randomized order up to 4
          trial_alphas = rng.shuffle? np.random has shuffle. Use np.array and rng.choice without replace.
          for a in chosen:
             th=cur_theta - a*dir
             pt=_softmax(th)
             s_try=_score_L(pt,L)
             if s_try < sc - 1e-12:
                cur_theta=th; cur_score=s_try; improved=True
                if cur_score < best_score: best=pt; best_theta=th.copy(); best_score=cur_score
                break
          if improved: break
       if improved: stall=0
       else:
          stall +=1
          if stall > 10:
             # restore if stuck
             if cur_score > best_score*1.01:
                cur_theta=best_theta.copy(); cur_score=best_score
             # random noise
             noise = float(rng.choice([0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2]))
             th=best_theta + rng.normal(0, noise, n)
             th-=th.mean()
             pt=_softmax(th); s_try=_score_L(pt,L)
             if np.isfinite(s_try) and s_try < min(cur_score, best_score*1.05):
                 cur_theta=th; cur_score=s_try; stall=0
             else:
                 stall=0
       # maybe periodic spectral? no
    return best_p,best_score
```

Let's write `_conv_self` carefully:
```
def _conv_self(a, L=None):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    n=len(a)
    nout=2*n-1
    if n==0: return np.array([])
    if nout == 1: return a*a
    if n*n <= 1000000:
       out=np.convolve(a,a)
    else:
       if L is None: L=_next_pow2(nout)
       out=np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(a,L)*np.fft.rfft(a,L),L)
       out=out[:nout]
    out=np.where(np.isfinite(out), out, 0.0)
    out=np.maximum(out,0.0)
    return out
```
Clipping max(0) for nonnegative. For scoring, if true small negative due to roundoff, okay. But for active direction, p positive, A from q positive; weighted_A can also clip negative.

`_weighted_A`:
```
def _weighted_A(q,p,L):
   n=len(p)
   if L is None or L < 2*n-1: L=_next_pow2(2*n-1)
   Q=np.zeros(L); Q[:len(q)]=q[:L]
   P=np.zeros(L); P[:n]=p
   R=np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(Q,L)*np.conj(np.fft.rfft(P,L)),L)
   R=R[:n]
   R=np.where(np.isfinite(R), R,0.0)
   R=np.maximum(R,0.0)
   return R
```
Is q length maybe 2n-1 exactly; L>=q length. Good.

Could precompute rfft(P) within active iterations? p changes, no.

cmax=max(c)

# ensure C_new symmetric? c symmetric, but due rounding; sym: C_new=(C_new + C_new[::-1])/2

s=a.sum(); if s<1e-300: None
return a/s

Let's draft code:

```
"""
Searches ... uses smooth log-domain conjugate gradient/L-BFGS and exact active-set multiplicative line search.
"""
import numpy as np
import time
try:
    from scipy.optimize import minimize
except Exception:
    minimize = None
```

Functions:
```
def _next_pow2(x):
    x=int(max(1,x))
    L=1
    while L<x: L <<=1
    return L
```

`_sanitize`:
```
def _sanitize(p):
    try: arr=np.asarray(p,dtype=float)
    except: return None
    if arr.size==0: return None
    arr=arr.reshape(-1)
    arr=np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr=np.maximum(arr,0.0)
    # avoid elements >1000? official clamps; for score, schema? But if parent has large? normalize.
    arr=np.minimum(arr,1000.0)
    s=arr.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s<1e-300: return None
    return arr/s
```
Should we cap at 1000 before normalize? If sequence all 2000, official caps to1000 then scale invariant; normalize after cap okay. For candidate, no issue.

`_softmax_theta(theta)`:
```
def _softmax_theta(theta):
    theta=np.asarray(theta,float)
    theta=np.where(np.isfinite(theta),theta,0.0)
    theta=np.clip(theta,-80.0,80.0)
    m=np.max(theta)
    u=np.exp(theta-m)
    s=u.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s<=0:
        return np.ones(len(theta))/len(theta)
    return u/s
```

`_conv_self` as above.

`_score_L`:
```
def _score_L(p, L=None):
    arr=np.asarray(p,float)
    arr=np.where(np.isfinite(arr),arr,0.0); arr=np.maximum(arr,0.0)
    s=arr.sum()
    if s<1e-300: return float('inf')
    arr=arr/s
    n=len(arr)
    if L is None: L=_next_pow2(2*n-1)
    c=_conv_self(arr,L)
    if c.size==0: return float('inf')
    mx=float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(mx): return float('inf')
    return float(2.0*n*mx)
```
Could be more efficient if p already normalized; but OK. In active refine, calls many times; sanitizing each candidate (sum, finite) overhead. Let's make `_score_normalized(p,L)` assuming p normalized nonnegative finite:
```
def _score_normalized(p, L):
    n=len(p)
    c=_conv_self(p,L)
    return 2*n*c.max()
```

`_smooth_f_g(theta,beta,reg,L)`: Need if L not matching len, recompute. Use `n=len(theta)`, if L<2n-1 set L.
```
def _smooth_f_g(theta, beta, reg, L):
   theta=np.asarray(theta,float)
   theta=np.where(np.isfinite(theta),theta,0.0)
   n=len(theta)
   if L is None or L<2*n-1: L=_next_pow2(2*n-1)
   # softmax
   m=np.max(theta)
   u=np.exp(theta-m)
   s=u.sum()
   if not ...: p=uniform
   else: p=u/s
   c=_conv_self(p,L)
   d=n*c
   shift=d.max(); z=beta*(d-shift); z=np.clip(z,-700,700); e=np.exp(z); es=e.sum()
   if not finite es or es==0: q=zeros; q[d.argmax()]=1; lse=shift
   else: q=e/es; lse=shift + math.log(es)/beta
   A=_weighted_A(q,p,L)
   D=float(np.dot(p,A))
   g=2.0*n*beta*p*(A-D)
   if reg !=0:
      f = lse + 0.5*reg*float(np.dot(theta,theta))
      g = g + reg*theta
   else: f=lse
   return float(f), g.astype(float)
```
If beta is negative? no.
If beta very small (e.g., 0) log es? We use beta>1.
Potential problem: f includes shift; if beta high and shift large, f ~ shift. Good.

`_gradient_cost(p,L)`:
```
def _gradient_cost(p,L):
    theta=np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300)); theta-=theta.mean()
    t=time.monotonic()
    _smooth_f_g(theta, 100.0, 1e-10, L)
    return max(1e-5, time.monotonic()-t)
```

`_run_lbfgs(p0,betas,time_budget,verbose=False)`:
```
def _run_lbfgs(p0,betas,time_budget,verbose=False):
    p=_sanitize(p0)
    if p is None: return None,float('inf')
    n=len(p); L=_next_pow2(2*n-1)
    theta=np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300)); theta-=theta.mean()
    best_p=p.copy(); best_score=_score_sum1(p,L)
    best_theta=theta.copy()
    deadline=time.monotonic()+time_budget
    if minimize is None: return best_p,best_score
    if len(betas)==0: return...
    cost=_gradient_cost(p,L)
    if n<=4096: cap=350
    elif n<=8192: cap=160
    elif n<=16384: cap=80
    else: cap=40
    bounds=[(-50.0,50.0)]*n
    options_base={'maxcor':10,'maxls':10,'ftol':1e-12,'gtol':1e-6}
    for beta in betas:
       if time.monotonic()>=deadline: break
       rem=deadline-time.monotonic()
       # also reserve for future betas? maybe use rem; but if first beta takes all, later none. We can divide by remaining_count.
       rem_per=rem/ (len(betas)-idx) ??? Need idx. Use loop enumerate.
       maxiter=int(rem_per / (8.0*cost))
       maxiter=max(10, min(cap,maxiter))
       options=dict(options_base); options['maxiter']=maxiter
       try: res=minimize(...)
       except: break
       if res.x finite:
          theta=np.asarray(res.x,float); theta=np.where(finite,theta,0); theta-=theta.mean()
          theta=np.clip(theta,-50,50)? bounds already, but maybe.
          p_try=_softmax_theta(theta)
          sc=_score_sum1(p_try,L)
          if np.isfinite(sc):
             cur_score=sc
             if sc<best_score-1e-12: best...
             # if current much worse, next beta from best?
             if best_score < cur_score*1.03:
                 theta=best_theta.copy()
             else:
                 theta=theta.copy()
          else: theta=best_theta
       else: theta=best_theta
    # final: maybe exact active? not here
    return best_p,best_score
```
If beta schedule length, division: We need index. Use `for bi,beta in enumerate(betas): remaining_count=len(betas)-bi`. `rem_per = rem / max(1, remaining_count)` to ensure all beta get some time. But if early beta makes no progress, okay.
`cost` estimated per gradient; L-BFGS iteration may call more; 8 factor. For many betas, maxiter small. Example rem=400, betas=5, cost=0.002 -> rem_per first=80, maxiter=80/(0.016)=5000 cap 350. All 5*350. Good. Time maybe fine.
Set `gtol=1e-5` maybe to stop if stationary; but with maxiter. Use 1e-6.

If L-BFGS fails due to `maxiter` too small, still maybe improve.

Potential issue: `minimize` returns `fun` final not used. It may change theta to a point with worse exact but better smooth; for next beta, starting worse exact might go to similar basin. We can start next beta from best exact to be safe, but smooth maybe. Use if current score > best_score*1.1 reset to best; else continue current.

`_active_refine`:
Let's write robust using `_score_sum1` and `_conv_self` once for current p to get d for directions and score. Need score from c: sc=2n c.max. Use `_direction_from_q(q,p,L)`:
```
def _gradient_direction(q,p,L):
    A=_weighted_A(q,p,L)
    D=float(np.dot(p,A))
    dir=A-D
    maxabs=float(np.max(np.abs(dir)))
    if maxabs>1e-14: dir=dir/maxabs
    else: return None
    return dir
```
For one-sided use different.

Top-level `_active_refine(p0,rng,time_budget,verbose=False)`:
```
def _active_refine(p0,rng,time_budget,verbose=False):
   p=_sanitize(p0); if None...
   n=len(p); L=...
   theta=np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300)); theta-=mean
   best_p=p; best_score=_score_sum1(p,L); best_theta=theta
   cur_theta=theta.copy(); deadline=...
   alphas=np.array([0.01,0.03,0.1,0.3,1.0,3.0,10.0])
   K_choices=np.array([1,2,4,8,16,32,64,128,256])
   stall=0
   it=0
   while time.monotonic()<deadline:
      p_cur=_softmax_theta(cur_theta)
      p_cur-=mean? softmax already sum1.
      c=_conv_self(p_cur,L); score=sum?; if score<best update
      d=n*c
      if not finite: break
      directions=[]
      # uniform q
      q_uniform? This costs weighted_A (quasi uniform) no partition. Add if it<... Maybe always:
      A=_weighted_A(np.ones(len(d))/len(d),p_cur,L); dir=A-dot(p,A); normalize; directions.append
      # softmax q
      beta = exp uniform log
      q=softmax; dir append
      # topK
      K=choice; append
      # one-sided peak if rng
      # limit to 3 directions to save: maybe choose random subset
      # line search
      improved=False
      for dir in directions:
         alphas_try = alphas[rng.choice(len(alphas), size=4, replace=False)]
         for a in alphas_try:
            th=cur_theta - a*dir
            th-=th.mean()
            pt=_softmax_theta(th)
            sc=_score_sum1(pt,L)
            if sc < score - 1e-12:
                cur_theta=th; score=sc; improved=True
                if sc<best_score: best...
                break
         if improved: break
      if improved: stall=0
      else:
         stall +=1
         if stall>8:
            if score > best_score*1.015:
               cur_theta=best_theta.copy(); score=best_score
            noise_std = float(rng.choice([0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4]))
            st=best_theta + rng.normal(0.0,noise_std,n); st-=st.mean(); st=np.clip(st,-50,50)
            pt=_softmax_theta(st); sc=_score_sum1(pt,L)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < min(score, best_score*1.05):
                cur_theta=st; score=sc
                # don't update best unless improves
                stall=0
            else:
                stall=0
      it +=1
   return best_p,best_score
```
Uniform q direction: For p normalized, A uniform q can be computed via sliding sum; but weighted_A with q length large O(FFT). OK but adds cost. We can skip uniform to save; maybe use softmax q with low beta approximates. We'll include softmax, topK, one-sided.

Need max directions memory small.

For max reduction, direction derivative of weighted Q =2n p(A-D). Gradient descent θ -= α (A-D) (since gθ factor). Yes. But if D large? Direction sign correct. Maybe sometimes θ += αdir improves exact due to nonconvex; line search negative α? We can also try `th=cur_theta + a*dir` for a small maybe. Add for each dir try both signs for first alpha? Too many. We can if no improvement, try one positive alpha. Simpler: after negative alphas fail, try `th=cur_theta + a*dir` for a in [0.01,0.05,0.1]. This can escape.

Let's include `_spectral_flatten(p, alpha, L)`:
```
def _spectral_flatten(p, alpha, L):
    p=np.asarray(p,float); n=len(p)
    if L is None or L<2*n-1: L=_next_pow2(2*n-1)
    c=_conv_self(p,L)
    cmax=float(c.max())
    if cmax <=0 or not finite: return None
    C=c/cmax
    C=np.where(C>0,C,0)
    C_new=np.power(C, 1.0/alpha) # scaled cmax later only ratio matters? If scale not matter for spectral? Need scale to preserve sum? We can multiply by cmax to keep magnitude.
    C_new*=cmax
    # sym enforce
    C_new=0.5*(C_new + C_new[::-1])
    F=np.fft.rfft(C_new,L).astype(complex)
    # Remove tiny imaginary? keep.
    factor=np.sqrt(F)
    a=np.fft.irfft(factor,L)[:n]
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a),a,0)
    negfrac=float(np.mean(a<0))
    if negfrac > 0.35: return None
    a=np.maximum(a,0)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<1e-300: return None
    return a/s
```
If F has negative real and any imag, sqrt complex gives complex; irfft should produce real if F Hurwitz? For full complex with conjugate symmetry? `rfft(C)` has conjugate symmetry? For real C, rfft values represent conjugate pairs. `sqrt(F)` may break conjugate symmetry? For k, conj? If C real, F[k] has conjugate F[-k]. sqrt of each positive branch: sqrt(conj z)=conj sqrt(z) if branch principal? Yes for not crossing negative real? It preserves conjugate symmetry mostly. irfft of rfft-like? We are passing full complex array of length L//2+1 from rfft(C); factor length same. irfft expects Hermitian; sqrt preserves. OK.
The scale C_new can be arbitrary; sum p normalized after.

Could flatten with target **not c^1/alpha** but mix current c and flat: `C_new = (1-lam)*c + lam*cmax*ones/L`. This may be smoother. Add optional.
`_spectral_flatten_mix(p, mix, L)`:
```
C=c; C_mix = (1-mix)*C + mix*(C.sum()/len(C))*np.ones; # average = sum C/(2n-1)=1/(2n-1) since p sum1. scale? Use cmax?```

```
def _spectral_flatten(p, mode, L):
   c=conv; cmax; flat=cmean
   if mode=='power': target=cmax*(c/cmax)**(1/power)
   if mode=='mix': target=(1-mix)c + mix*flat
   if mode='log': target = cmax*(c/cmax)**(1/power) maybe
```

Use top-level `_dir_from_q`:
```
def _dir_from_q(q,p,L):
    A=_weighted_A(q,p,L)
    D=float(np.dot(p,A))
    dirv=A-D
    norm=float(np.max(np.abs(dirv)))
    if not np.isfinite(norm) or norm<=1e-14: return None
    return dirv/norm
```

def _active_refine(...):
    p0sanitize; n L
    cur_theta=...
    best_p=p.copy(); best_score=_score_sum1(p,L); best_theta=cur_theta.copy()
    cur_score=best_score
    deadline=...
    alphas=...
    K_choices=...
    stall=0; it=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        p_cur=_softmax_theta(cur_theta)
        c=_conv_self(p_cur,L)
        score=float(2*n*c.max())
        if not finite: break
        if score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_p=p_cur.copy(); best_score=score; best_theta=cur_theta.copy(); cur_score=score
        d=n*c
        improved_this_iter=False
        # sym occasionally
        if it % 8 == 4:
           ps=0.5*(p_cur+p_cur[::-1]); ss=ps.sum(); if ss>...: ps/=ss
           scs=_score_sum1(ps,L)
           if finite and scs < score - 1e-12:
               score=scs; cur_theta=logsoftmax ps; improved_this_iter=True; if score<best: update
        # spectral occasionally
        if not improved_this_iter and (stall > 12 or it % 37 == 0):
           alpha_f=...
           pf=_spectral_flatten(p_cur, alpha_f, L)
           if pf is not None:
              scf=_score_sum1(pf,L)
              if finite and scf < score - 1e-12:
                 score=scf; cur_theta=log pf; improved=True; update
        if not improved_this_iter:
           directions=[]
           # softmax q
           beta_gen = float(10.0 ** rng.uniform(0.5, 4.0))
           q=_softmax_lags(d,beta_gen)
           dirv=_dir_from_q(q,p_cur,L); if dirv is not None: directions.append(dirv)
           K=...
           q2=_topk_weights(d,K); dirv2=...; if not None append
           if rng.random()<0.4:
              mstar=argmax(d); A3=...; crit=...; dir3=...; append
           if directions:
              # limit 3
              if len(directions)>3: directions=directions[:3]
              for dirv in directions:
                 order=rng.permutation(len(alphas))[:min(4,len(alphas))]
                 for idx in order:
                    a=float(alphas[idx])
                    th=cur_theta - a*dirv
                    th-=th.mean()
                    pt=_softmax_theta(th)
                    sc_try=_score_sum1(pt,L)
                    if finite and sc_try < score - 1e-12:
                       cur_theta=th; score=sc_try; improved_this_iter=True
                       if score < best_score -1e-12: update
                       break
                 if improved_this_iter: break
        if improved_this_iter:
           stall=0
        else:
           stall +=1
           if stall > 10:
              if score > best_score * 1.01 + 1e-12:
                  cur_theta=best_theta.copy(); score=best_score
                  p_cur=_softmax_theta(cur_theta)
              noise_std=float(rng.choice([0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4]))
              th=best_theta + rng.normal(0.0, noise_std, n)
              th-=th.mean(); th=np.clip(th,-60,60)
              pt=_softmax_theta(th); sc_try=_score_sum1(pt,L)
              if finite and sc_try < min(score, best_score*1.05 + 1e-12):
                  cur_theta=th; score=sc_try
              stall=0
        it +=1
        if verbose and it%200==0: print
    return best_p,best_score
```

```python
"""
Search for non-negative coefficient sequences minimizing
    2*n*max(conv(a,a)) / sum(a)^2

The program combines:
  1) fast FFT-based exact scoring,
  2) log-domain L-BFGS optimization of a logarithmic-sum-of-expaces smoothing
     of the convolution peak,
  3) exact active-set / multiplicative line searches in the log-domain,
  4) diverse resampling and restarts across several sequence lengths,
  5) occasional spectral-flattening moves to escape local basins.

All helper functions are top-level and contain no closures or lambdas.
"""

import math
import time

import numpy as np

try:
    from scipy.optimize import minimize
except Exception:
    minimize = None


def _score_key(item):
    return item[1]


def _next_pow2(x):
    x = int(max(1, x))
    out = 1
    while out < x:
        out <<= 1
    return out


def _sanitize(p):
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(p, dtype=float)
    except Exception:
        return None
    arr = arr.reshape(-1)
    if arr.size == 0:
        return None
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.minimum(arr, 1000.0)
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-300:
        return None
    return arr / s


def _softmax_theta(theta):
    theta = np.asarray(theta, dtype=float)
    theta = np.where(np.isfinite(theta), theta, 0.0)
    theta = np.clip(theta, -80.0, 80.0)
    m = float(np.max(theta))
    u = np.exp(theta - m)
    s = float(u.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(theta.size, dtype=float) / max(1, theta.size)
    return u / s


def _conv_self(a, L=None):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=float)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=float)
    if n == 1:
        return np.array([a[0] * a[0]], dtype=float)
    nout = 2 * n - 1
    if n * n <= 1000000:
        out = np.convolve(a, a)
    else:
        if L is None or L < nout:
            L = _next_pow2(nout)
        out = np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(a, L) * np.fft.rfft(a, L), L)
        out = out[:nout]
    out = np.where(np.isfinite(out), out, 0.0)
    out = np.maximum(out, 0.0)
    return out


def _score_sum1(p, L=None):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=float)
    n = int(p.size)
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return float("inf")
    if abs(s - 1.0) > 1e-6:
        p = p / s
    if L is None or L < (2 * n - 1):
        L = _next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    c = _conv_self(p, L)
    if c.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    mx = float(np.max(c))
    if not math.isfinite(mx):
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * n * mx)


def _score(p):
    p = _sanitize(p)
    if p is None:
        return float("inf")
    return _score_sum1(p)


def _weighted_A(q, p, L):
    n = int(p.size)
    if L is None or L < (2 * n - 1):
        L = _next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    Q = np.zeros(L, dtype=float)
    Q[:int(q.size)] = q[:int(q.size)]
    P = np.zeros(L, dtype=float)
    P[:n] = p
    R = np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(Q, L) * np.conj(np.fft.rfft(P, L)), L)
    R = R[:n]
    R = np.where(np.isfinite(R), R, 0.0)
    R = np.maximum(R, 0.0)
    return R


def _softmax_lags(d, beta):
    d = np.asarray(d, dtype=float)
    shift = float(np.max(d))
    z = beta * (d - shift)
    z = np.clip(z, -700.0, 700.0)
    e = np.exp(z)
    es = float(e.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(es) or es <= 1e-300:
        q = np.zeros_like(d, dtype=float)
        q[int(np.argmax(d))] = 1.0
        return q
    return e / es


def _topk_weights(d, K):
    d = np.asarray(d, dtype=float)
    m = int(d.size)
    K = int(min(max(1, K), m))
    if K <= 1:
        q = np.zeros(m, dtype=float)
        q[int(np.argmax(d))] = 1.0
        return q
    idx = np.argpartition(d, -K)[-K:]
    vals = d[idx]
    order = np.argsort(vals)[::-1]
    idx = idx[order]
    ranks = np.arange(K, dtype=float)
    w = np.exp(-ranks / max(1.0, 0.35 * K))
    w = w / w.sum()
    q = np.zeros(m, dtype=float)
    q[idx] = w
    return q


def _dir_from_q(q, p, L):
    A = _weighted_A(q, p, L)
    D = float(np.dot(p, A))
    dirv = A - D
    norm = float(np.max(np.abs(dirv)))
    if not math.isfinite(norm) or norm <= 1e-14:
        return None
    return dirv / norm


def _smooth_f_g(theta, beta, reg, L):
    theta = np.asarray(theta, dtype=float)
    theta = np.where(np.isfinite(theta), theta, 0.0)
    theta = np.clip(theta, -80.0, 80.0)
    n = int(theta.size)
    if n == 0:
        return 0.0, np.array([], dtype=float)
    if L is None or L < (2 * n - 1):
        L = _next_pow2(2 * n - 1)

    m = float(np.max(theta))
    u = np.exp(theta - m)
    s = float(u.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        p = np.ones(n, dtype=float) / n
    else:
        p = u / s

    c = _conv_self(p, L)
    d = float(n) * c
    Ld = int(d.size)

    shift = float(np.max(d))
    beta = float(max(1e-6, beta))
    z = beta * (d - shift)
    z = np.clip(z, -700.0, 700.0)
    e = np.exp(z)
    es = float(e.sum())

    if not math.isfinite(es) or es <= 1e-300:
        q = np.zeros_like(d, dtype=float)
        q[int(np.argmax(d))] = 1.0
        f = shift - math.log(float(Ld)) / beta
    else:
        q = e / es
        f = shift + (math.log(es) - math.log(float(Ld))) / beta

    A = _weighted_A(q, p, L)
    D = float(np.dot(p, A))
    g = 2.0 * float(n) * p * (A - D)

    if reg != 0.0:
        f = f + 0.5 * reg * float(np.dot(theta, theta))
        g = g + reg * theta

    if not math.isfinite(f):
        f = 1e6
    g = np.where(np.isfinite(g), g, 0.0)
    return float(f), g.astype(float)


def _gradient_cost(p, L):
    theta = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
    if theta.size:
        theta = theta - float(np.mean(theta))
    t = time.monotonic()
    _smooth_f_g(theta, 100.0, 0.0, L)
    return max(1e-5, time.monotonic() - t)


def _run_lbfgs(p0, betas, time_budget, verbose=False):
    p = _sanitize(p0)
    if p is None:
        return None, float("inf")
    n = int(p.size)
    if n == 0:
        return None, float("inf")
    L = _next_pow2(2 * n - 1)

    theta = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
    theta = theta - float(np.mean(theta))

    best_p = p.copy()
    best_theta = theta.copy()
    best_score = _score_sum1(p, L)
    cur_score = best_score

    if minimize is None:
        return best_p, best_score
    if len(betas) == 0 or time_budget <= 1.0:
        return best_p, best_score

    cost = _gradient_cost(p, L)
    if cost <= 0.0 or not math.isfinite(cost):
        cost = 0.002

    if n <= 4096:
        iter_cap = 400
    elif n <= 8192:
        iter_cap = 180
    elif n <= 16384:
        iter_cap = 90
    else:
        iter_cap = 50

    bounds = [(-50.0, 50.0)] * n
    deadline = time.monotonic() + float(time_budget)

    for bi, beta in enumerate(betas):
        tnow = time.monotonic()
        if tnow >= deadline:
            break
        rem = deadline - tnow
        rem_per = rem / float(len(betas) - bi)
        # Conservative scaling: L-BFGS may perform many function calls per iteration.
        maxiter = int(rem_per / max(1e-6, 20.0 * cost))
        maxiter = max(10, min(iter_cap, maxiter))

        opts = {
            "maxiter": maxiter,
            "ftol": 1e-11,
            "gtol": 1e-6,
            "maxcor": 10,
            "maxls": 10,
        }

        try:
            res = minimize(
                _smooth_f_g,
                theta,
                method="L-BFGS-B",
                jac=True,
                args=(float(beta), 1e-10, L),
                bounds=bounds,
                options=opts,
            )
            x = getattr(res, "x", None)
            if x is not None:
                theta2 = np.asarray(x, dtype=float)
                theta2 = np.where(np.isfinite(theta2), theta2, 0.0)
                theta2 = np.clip(theta2, -50.0, 50.0)
                theta2 = theta2 - float(np.mean(theta2))
                p2 = _softmax_theta(theta2)
                s2 = _score_sum1(p2, L)
                if math.isfinite(s2):
                    if s2 < best_score - 1e-12:
                        best_p = p2.copy()
                        best_theta = theta2.copy()
                        best_score = float(s2)
                        cur_score = best_score
                    else:
                        if cur_score > best_score * 1.15:
                            theta = best_theta.copy()
                        else:
                            theta = theta2.copy()
                else:
                    theta = best_theta.copy()
            else:
                theta = best_theta.copy()
        except Exception:
            theta = best_theta.copy()

        if verbose:
            print(f"      LBFGS beta={float(beta):.3g}: best={best_score:.6f}")

    return best_p, best_score


def _spectral_flatten(p, alpha, L):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=float)
    n = int(p.size)
    if n == 0:
        return None
    if L is None or L < (2 * n - 1):
        L = _next_pow2(2 * n - 1)

    c = _conv_self(p, L)
    cmax = float(np.max(c))
    if not math.isfinite(cmax) or cmax <= 1e-300:
        return None

    C = c / cmax
    C = np.where(C > 0.0, C, 0.0)
    C_new = cmax * np.power(C, 1.0 / float(alpha))
    C_new = 0.5 * (C_new + C_new[::-1])

    F = np.fft.rfft(C_new, L).astype(complex)
    factor = np.sqrt(F)
    factor[0] = abs(factor[0])
    if L % 2 == 0:
        factor[-1] = abs(factor[-1])

    a = np.fft.irfft(factor, L)[:n]
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)

    negfrac = float(np.mean(a < 0.0))
    if negfrac > 0.35:
        return None
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return None
    return a / s


def _init_uniform(n):
    return np.ones(int(n), dtype=float) / float(n)


def _init_two_level(n, rng):
    n = int(n)
    k = int(n * rng.uniform(0.30, 0.70))
    ratio = float(rng.uniform(1.4, 5.0))
    p = np.ones(n, dtype=float)
    p[k:] = ratio
    p = p[rng.permutation(n)]
    p = p * (1.0 + 0.08 * rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, n))
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return _init_uniform(n)
    return p / s


def _init_gauss_mixture(n, rng):
    n = int(n)
    K = int(rng.integers(2, 8))
    p = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    t = np.arange(n, dtype=float) / float(n)
    for _ in range(K):
        c = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 1.0))
        s = float(rng.uniform(0.02, 0.28))
        w = float(rng.uniform(0.4, 2.0))
        x = (t - c) / s
        x = np.minimum(np.abs(x), 1.0 - np.abs(x))
        p += w * np.exp(-0.5 * x * x)
    p = np.maximum(p, 1e-12)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return _init_uniform(n)
    return p / s


def _init_cosine(n, rng):
    n = int(n)
    t = np.arange(n, dtype=float) / float(n)
    p = np.ones(n, dtype=float)
    for k in range(1, 10):
        amp = float(rng.uniform(-0.55, 0.55)) / float(k)
        ph = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        p += amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * k * t + ph)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return _init_uniform(n)
    return p / s


def _init_block_random(n, rng):
    n = int(n)
    K = int(rng.integers(4, 20))
    p = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    edges = np.sort(rng.uniform(0.0, 1.0, max(1, K - 1)))
    blocks = np.concatenate(([0.0], edges, [1.0]))
    heights = rng.lognormal(0.0, 1.0, K)
    for i in range(K):
        a = int(round(blocks[i] * n))
        b = int(round(blocks[i + 1] * n))
        b = max(b, a + 1)
        b = min(n, b)
        p[a:b] = heights[i]
    p = np.maximum(p, 1e-12)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return _init_uniform(n)
    return p / s


def _init_sparse_sum(n, rng):
    n = int(n)
    s = int(max(16, n * rng.uniform(0.05, 0.35)))
    s = min(s, n)
    idx = rng.choice(n, size=s, replace=False)
    w = rng.lognormal(0.0, 1.0, s)
    p = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    p[idx] = w
    p = np.maximum(p, 1e-18)
    ssum = float(p.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(ssum) or ssum <= 1e-300:
        return _init_uniform(n)
    return p / ssum


def _resample(p_in, n_new, rng=None):
    p = _sanitize(p_in)
    if p is None:
        return None
    n_old = int(p.size)
    n_new = int(n_new)
    if n_old == n_new:
        out = p.copy()
    elif n_old == 1:
        out = np.ones(n_new, dtype=float)
    else:
        x_old = (np.arange(n_old, dtype=float) + 0.5) / float(n_old)
        x_new = (np.arange(n_new, dtype=float) + 0.5) / float(n_new)
        out = np.interp(x_new, x_old, p)
    out = np.maximum(out, 0.0)
    if rng is not None:
        out = out * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.03, n_new))
        out = np.maximum(out, 0.0)
    s = float(out.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return None
    return out / s


def _lbfgs(betas, time_budget, p, rng, verbose=False):
    p_out, sc_out = _run_lbfgs(p, betas, time_budget, verbose=verbose)
    return p_out, sc_out


def _polish(p, rng, time_budget):
    return _active_refine(p, rng, time_budget)


def _active_refine(p0, rng, time_budget, verbose=False):
    p = _sanitize(p0)
    if p is None:
        return None, float("inf")
    n = int(p.size)
    if n == 0:
        return None, float("inf")
    if time_budget <= 0.5:
        return p.copy(), _score_sum1(p)

    L = _next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    cur_theta = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
    cur_theta = cur_theta - float(np.mean(cur_theta))

    best_p = p.copy()
    best_theta = cur_theta.copy()
    best_score = _score_sum1(p, L)
    cur_score = best_score

    alphas = np.array([0.01, 0.03, 0.10, 0.30, 1.0, 3.0, 8.0], dtype=float)
    k_choices = np.array([1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32, 64, 128, 256], dtype=np.int64)

    deadline = time.monotonic() + float(time_budget)
    stall = 0
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        p_cur = _softmax_theta(cur_theta)
        c = _conv_self(p_cur, L)
        score = float(2.0 * n * c.max())
        if not math.isfinite(score):
            break
        d = float(n) * c

        if score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_p = p_cur.copy()
            best_theta = cur_theta.copy()
            best_score = float(score)
            cur_score = best_score

        improved = False

        # Occasional symmetrization.
        if it % 9 == 5:
            ps = 0.5 * (p_cur + p_cur[::-1])
            ss = float(ps.sum())
            if math.isfinite(ss) and ss > 1e-300:
                ps = ps / ss
                scs = _score_sum1(ps, L)
                if math.isfinite(scs) and scs < score - 1e-11:
                    score = float(scs)
                    cur_theta = np.log(np.maximum(ps, 1e-300))
                    cur_theta = cur_theta - float(np.mean(cur_theta))
                    improved = True
                    if score < best_score - 1e-12:
                        best_p = ps.copy()
                        best_theta = cur_theta.copy()
                        best_score = float(score)
                        cur_score = best_score

        # Occasional spectral flatten.
        if not improved and (stall > 12 or it % 41 == 0):
            alpha_f = float(rng.choice([1.25, 1.6, 2.2, 3.5, 6.0, 12.0]))
            pf = _spectral_flatten(p_cur, alpha_f, L)
            if pf is not None:
                scf = _score_sum1(pf, L)
                if math.isfinite(scf) and scf < score - 1e-11:
                    score = float(scf)
                    cur_theta = np.log(np.maximum(pf, 1e-300))
                    cur_theta = cur_theta - float(np.mean(cur_theta))
                    improved = True
                    if score < best_score - 1e-12:
                        best_p = pf.copy()
                        best_theta = cur_theta.copy()
                        best_score = float(score)
                        cur_score = best_score

        # Active-set / soft-max directions.
        if not improved:
            directions = []

            beta_gen = float(10.0 ** rng.uniform(0.5, 4.0))
            q1 = _softmax_lags(d, beta_gen)
            dir1 = _dir_from_q(q1, p_cur, L)
            if dir1 is not None:
                directions.append(dir1)

            K = int(rng.choice(k_choices))
            q2 = _topk_weights(d, K)
            dir2 = _dir_from_q(q2, p_cur, L)
            if dir2 is not None:
                directions.append(dir2)

            if rng.random() < 0.45:
                mstar = int(np.argmax(d))
                q3 = np.zeros_like(d, dtype=float)
                q3[mstar] = 1.0
                A3 = _weighted_A(q3, p_cur, L)
                crit = 0.60 * float(np.max(A3)) if A3.size else 0.0
                dir3 = np.maximum(A3 - crit, 0.0)
                nm3 = float(np.max(np.abs(dir3)))
                if nm3 > 1e-14:
                    directions.append(dir3 / nm3)

            if len(directions) > 3:
                directions = directions[:3]

            for dirv in directions:
                take = min(4, len(alphas))
                order = rng.permutation(len(alphas))[:take]
                for j in order:
                    a = float(alphas[int(j)])
                    th = cur_theta - a * dirv
                    th = th - float(np.mean(th))
                    th = np.clip(th, -70.0, 70.0)
                    pt = _softmax_theta(th)
                    s_try = _score_sum1(pt, L)
                    if math.isfinite(s_try) and s_try < score - 1e-12:
                        cur_theta = th
                        cur_score = float(s_try)
                        score = cur_score
                        improved = True
                        if cur_score < best_score - 1e-12:
                            best_p = pt.copy()
                            best_theta = cur_theta.copy()
                            best_score = cur_score
                        break
                if improved:
                    break

        if improved:
            stall = 0
        else:
            stall += 1
            if stall > 10:
                if best_score < float("inf") and cur_score > best_score * 1.015:
                    cur_theta = best_theta.copy()
                    cur_score = best_score
                    score = cur_score

                noise_std = float(rng.choice([0.02, 0.05, 0.10, 0.20, 0.40]))
                th = best_theta + rng.normal(0.0, noise_std, n)
                th = th - float(np.mean(th))
                th = np.clip(th, -70.0, 70.0)
                pt = _softmax_theta(th)
                s_try = _score_sum1(pt, L)
                if math.isfinite(s_try) and s_try < min(cur_score, best_score * 1.05):
                    cur_theta = th
                    cur_score = float(s_try)
                stall = 0

        it += 1
        if verbose and (it % 200 == 0):
            print(f"      active n={n} it={it} best={best_score:.6f}")

    return best_p, best_score


def propose_candidate(seed=12345, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Returns a non-negative sequence attempting to beat the target upper bound.
    """
    t_start = time.monotonic()
    try:
        budget = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget = 2400.0
    if not math.isfinite(budget) or budget <= 120.0:
        budget = 2400.0

    soft_deadline = t_start + max(120.0, budget - 45.0)
    try:
        iseed = int(seed)
    except Exception:
        iseed = 12345
    rng = np.random.default_rng((iseed % (2**31 - 1)) or 0)

    try:
        parent = _sanitize(np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=float))
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    if parent is not None:
        sc_parent = _score(parent)
        print(f"parent score={sc_parent:.6f} n={len(parent)}")

    # Candidate pool.
    cands = []

    length_set = []
    if parent is not None:
        length_set.append(int(parent.size))
    for n in [3072, 4096, 8192]:
        length_set.append(n)
    if budget > 1600.0:
        length_set.append(16384)

    length_set = sorted(set([x for x in length_set if x >= 256]))
    if len(length_set) > 4:
        length_set = length_set[:4]

    print("Phase 1: initial candidates:", length_set)
    for n in length_set:
        init_items = []
        if parent is not None:
            pr = _resample(parent, n, rng)
            if pr is not None:
                init_items.append((n, pr))
        init_items.append((n, _init_uniform(n)))
        init_items.append((n, _init_two_level(n, rng)))
        init_items.append((n, _init_gauss_mixture(n, rng)))
        init_items.append((n, _init_cosine(n, rng)))
        init_items.append((n, _init_block_random(n, rng)))
        init_items.append((n, _init_sparse_sum(n, rng)))

        for _, p in init_items:
            p = _sanitize(p)
            if p is None:
                continue
            sc = _score_sum1(p, _next_pow2(2 * p.size - 1))
            if math.isfinite(sc):
                cands.append((p, float(sc), int(n)))

    if len(cands) == 0:
        p = _init_uniform(4096)
        sc = _score(p)
        cands.append((p, sc, 4096))

    cands.sort(key=_score_key)
    best_p = cands[0][0].copy()
    best_score = float(cands[0][1])
    print(f"initial best={best_score:.6f} n={len(best_p)}")

    # Choose primary optimization targets: best few candidates, preferably different lengths.
    main_targets = []
    seen_lengths = []
    for item in cands:
        p_c, s_c, n_c = item
        if s_c > best_score + 0.09:
            break
        if len(seen_lengths) >= 4:
            break
        if n_c in seen_lengths:
            continue
        seen_lengths.append(n_c)
        main_targets.append((p_c, s_c, n_c))
    if len(main_targets) < 1:
        main_targets.append((best_p, best_score, int(best_p.size)))

    phase2_end = t_start + 0.58 * budget
    if phase2_end > soft_deadline - 600.0:
        phase2_end = soft_deadline - 600.0

    print("Phase 2: L-BFGS smoothing optimization")
    for i, (p_t, s_t, n_t) in enumerate(main_targets):
        tnow = time.monotonic()
        if tnow > phase2_end - 60:
            break
        alloc = phase2_end - tnow - 30
        if i == 0:
            alloc = min(alloc, 0.55 * budget)
        elif i == 1:
            alloc = min(alloc, 0.25 * budget)
        else:
            alloc = min(alloc, 0.15 * budget)
        if alloc < 60.0:
            continue

        if i == 0:
            betas = [20.0, 80.0, 300.0, 1200.0, 4000.0]
        elif i == 1:
            betas = [40.0, 200.0, 1000.0, 4000.0]
        else:
            betas = [80.0, 400.0, 2000.0]

        p2, s2 = _run_lbfgs(p_t, betas, alloc, verbose=False)
        if p2 is not None and math.isfinite(s2) and s2 < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_p, best_score = p2.copy(), float(s2)
            print(f"  L-BFGS new best={best_score:.6f} n={len(best_p)}")

        tnow = time.monotonic()
        if tnow < phase2_end - 40 and s2 < best_score * 1.05:
            p3, s3 = _active_refine(p2, rng, min(60.0, phase2_end - tnow - 10))
            if p3 is not None and math.isfinite(s3) and s3 < best_score - 1e-12:
                best_p, best_score = p3.copy(), float(s3)
                print(f"  active new best={best_score:.6f} n={len(best_p)}")

    phase3_end = t_start + 0.74 * budget
    if phase3_end > soft_deadline - 360.0:
        phase3_end = soft_deadline - 360.0

    print("Phase 3: diverse restarts")
    restarts = 0
    while time.monotonic() < phase3_end - 30 and restarts < 7:
        focus_n = int(best_p.size) if best_p is not None else 4096
        r = rng.random()
        p_try = None
        if r < 0.25:
            p_try = _init_two_level(focus_n, rng)
        elif r < 0.45:
            p_try = _init_gauss_mixture(focus_n, rng)
        elif r < 0.60:
            p_try = _init_cosine(focus_n, rng)
        elif r < 0.75:
            p_try = _resample(best_p, focus_n, rng) if best_p is not None else _init_uniform(focus_n)
        else:
            n_alt = focus_n if focus_n >= 8192 else int(min(16384, 2 * focus_n))
            p_try = _resample(best_p, n_alt, rng) if best_p is not None else _init_uniform(n_alt)

        if p_try is None:
            restarts += 1
            continue

        p_try = _sanitize(p_try)
        if p_try is None:
            restarts += 1
            continue

        sc_try = _score_sum1(p_try, _next_pow2(2 * p_try.size - 1))
        if math.isfinite(sc_try) and sc_try < best_score * 1.08:
            alloc = min(70.0, phase3_end - time.monotonic() - 10)
            if alloc > 25.0:
                p2, s2 = _run_lbfgs(p_try, [80.0, 500.0, 2500.0], alloc)
                if p2 is not None and math.isfinite(s2) and s2 < best_score - 1e-12:
                    best_p, best_score = p2.copy(), float(s2)
                    print(f"  restart new best={best_score:.6f} n={len(best_p)}")
        else:
            if math.isfinite(sc_try) and sc_try < best_score:
                best_p, best_score = p_try.copy(), float(sc_try)
                print(f"  restart init new best={best_score:.6f} n={len(best_p)}")
        restarts += 1

    print("Phase 4: final polishing")
    final_best_p = best_p
    final_best_score = best_score

    if final_best_p is not None and time.monotonic() < soft_deadline - 120.0:
        alloc = min(0.22 * budget, soft_deadline - time.monotonic() - 120.0)
        if alloc > 30.0:
            p2, s2 = _run_lbfgs(final_best_p, [500.0, 2000.0, 8000.0, 25000.0], alloc)
            if p2 is not None and math.isfinite(s2) and s2 < final_best_score - 1e-12:
                final_best_p, final_best_score = p2.copy(), float(s2)
                print(f"  final LBFGS best={final_best_score:.6f}")

    if final_best_p is not None and time.monotonic() < soft_deadline - 60.0:
        alloc = min(0.28 * budget, soft_deadline - time.monotonic() - 30.0)
        if alloc > 20.0:
            p2, s2 = _active_refine(final_best_p, rng, alloc, verbose=False)
            if p2 is not None and math.isfinite(s2) and s2 < final_best_score - 1e-12:
                final_best_p, final_best_score = p2.copy(), float(s2)
                print(f"  final active best={final_best_score:.6f}")

    if final_best_p is not None and time.monotonic() < soft_deadline - 60.0:
        alloc = min(0.14 * budget, soft_deadline - time.monotonic() - 20.0)
        if alloc > 20.0:
            p2, s2 = _run_lbfgs(final_best_p, [2000.0, 10000.0, 40000.0], alloc)
            if p2 is not None and math.isfinite(s2) and s2 < final_best_score - 1e-12:
                final_best_p, final_best_score = p2.copy(), float(s2)
                print(f"  second final LBFGS best={final_best_score:.6f}")

    if final_best_p is not None and time.monotonic() < soft_deadline - 20.0:
        alloc = min(0.16 * budget, soft_deadline - time.monotonic() - 5.0)
        if alloc > 10.0:
            p2, s2 = _active_refine(final_best_p, rng, alloc)
            if p2 is not None and math.isfinite(s2) and s2 < final_best_score - 1e-12:
                final_best_p, final_best_score = p2.copy(), float(s2)
                print(f"  second final active best={final_best_score:.6f}")

    if final_best_p is None:
        final_best_p = _init_uniform(4096)
        final_best_score = _score(final_best_p)

    out = _sanitize(final_best_p)
    if out is None:
        out = _init_uniform(4096)
    out = np.asarray(out, dtype=float)
    out = np.where(np.isfinite(out), out, 0.0)
    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(out.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
        out = _init_uniform(4096)
    else:
        out = out / s

    final_local = _score(out)
    print(f"local final score={final_local:.6f} n={len(out)}")

    # Verify with the official evaluator once, if available.
    try:
        if len(out) <= 20000:
            eval_score = float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in out]))
            if math.isfinite(eval_score):
                final_local = min(final_local, eval_score)
    except Exception:
        pass

    print(f"final reported score={final_local:.6f} n={len(out)}")
    print(f"total time={time.monotonic() - t_start:.1f}s")

    return [float(x) for x in out]
```
